1. Notes Example / You Try It

Section 2.1 - Average to Instant Rate

U2-S1-NOTE-EX01
Section 2.1 | Notes Example | Current | DOK 2

Example

Over $x=-2$ to $x=1$, what is the secant slope for $f(x)=- x^{3} + 2 x^{2} - 5$?

Organize the work so the rule, definition, or substitution can be followed line by line.

Answer: $-5$
Solution / scoring notes: Use $\frac{f(b)-f(a)}{b-a}$. Substitution and simplification give $-5$.
U2-S1-NOTE-YTI01
Section 2.1 | Notes You Try It | Current | DOK 2

You Try It

Over $x=-1$ to $x=0$, what is the secant slope for $f(x)=x \left(- 2 x^{2} - x + 4\right)$?

Record the final result in a complete mathematical statement.

Answer: $3$
Solution / scoring notes: Use $\frac{f(b)-f(a)}{b-a}$. Substitution and simplification give $3$.
U2-S1-NOTE-EX02
Section 2.1 | Notes Example | Current | DOK 2

Example

The table gives values near $x=-1$. Use the outer two rows to estimate the tangent slope at $x=-1$.
$x$$f(x)$
$- \frac{5}{4}$$6.875$
$-1$$5.0$
$- \frac{3}{4}$$3.375$

Organize the work so the rule, definition, or substitution can be followed line by line.

Answer: $-7$
Solution / scoring notes: Use the symmetric secant slope $\frac{f(- \frac{3}{4})-f(- \frac{5}{4})}{\frac{1}{2}}$; it equals $-7$.
U2-S1-NOTE-YTI02
Section 2.1 | Notes You Try It | Current | DOK 2

You Try It

The table gives values near $x=2$. Use the outer two rows to estimate the tangent slope at $x=2$.
$x$$f(x)$
$\frac{7}{4}$$1.3125$
$2$$2.0$
$\frac{9}{4}$$2.8125$

Record the final result in a complete mathematical statement.

Answer: $3$
Solution / scoring notes: Use the symmetric secant slope $\frac{f(\frac{9}{4})-f(\frac{7}{4})}{\frac{1}{2}}$; it equals $3$.
U2-S1-NOTE-EX03
Section 2.1 | Notes Example | Current | DOK 2

Example

For $f(x)=2 x^{2} + x + 4$ at $a=-2$, the right-hand secant slopes are shown. Estimate the instantaneous rate as $h$ shrinks.
$h$Secant slope on $[a,a+h]$
$1$$-5.0$
$\frac{1}{2}$$-6.0$
$\frac{1}{4}$$-6.5$

Organize the work so the rule, definition, or substitution can be followed line by line.

Answer: Approximately $-7$.
Solution / scoring notes: The secant slopes approach $-7$; this is the tangent-slope estimate.
U2-S1-NOTE-YTI03
Section 2.1 | Notes You Try It | Current | DOK 2

$h$Secant slope on $[a,a+h]$
$1$$9.0$
$\frac{1}{2}$$8.0$
$\frac{1}{4}$$7.5$

Record the final result in a complete mathematical statement.

Answer: Approximately $7$.

U2-S1-NOTE-EX04
Section 2.1 | Notes Example | Current | DOK 2

Example

A storage tank contains $V(t)=t^{2} + 2 t + 50$ liters after $t$ minutes. Find the average rate of change from $t=1$ to $t=3$, and state its units.

Organize the work so the rule, definition, or substitution can be followed line by line.

Answer: $6$ liters per minute.
Solution / scoring notes: Compute $\frac{V(b)-V(a)}{b-a}$; the result is $6$. The units are liters per minute.
U2-S1-NOTE-YTI04
Section 2.1 | Notes You Try It | Current | DOK 2

You Try It

A storage tank contains $V(t)=2 t^{2} + 3 t + 20$ liters after $t$ minutes. Find the average rate of change from $t=3$ to $t=5$, and state its units.

Record the final result in a complete mathematical statement.

Answer: $19$ liters per minute.
Solution / scoring notes: Compute $\frac{V(b)-V(a)}{b-a}$; the result is $19$. The units are liters per minute.

Section 2.2 - Derivative Definition and Notation

U2-S2-NOTE-EX01
Section 2.2 | Notes Example | Current | DOK 1

Example

In words, what does $f'(3)$ represent?

Organize the work so the rule, definition, or substitution can be followed line by line.

Answer: The instantaneous rate of change of $f$ at $x=3$, equivalently the slope of the tangent line there.
Solution / scoring notes: Derivative-at-a-point notation names a single tangent slope/instantaneous rate, not a product.
U2-S2-NOTE-YTI01
Section 2.2 | Notes You Try It | Current | DOK 1

You Try It

In words, what does $f'(9)$ represent?

Record the final result in a complete mathematical statement.

Answer: The instantaneous rate of change of $f$ at $x=9$, equivalently the slope of the tangent line there.
Solution / scoring notes: Derivative-at-a-point notation names a single tangent slope/instantaneous rate, not a product.
U2-S2-NOTE-EX02
Section 2.2 | Notes Example | Current | DOK 2

Example

Use the limit definition to find $f'(1)$ for $f(x)=2 x^{2} + x + 4$.

Organize the work so the rule, definition, or substitution can be followed line by line.

Answer: $5$
Solution / scoring notes: Set up $\lim_{h\to0}\frac{f(1+h)-f(1)}{h}$. Expand, cancel the factor $h$, then let $h\qquad o0$; the value is $5$.
U2-S2-NOTE-YTI02
Section 2.2 | Notes You Try It | Current | DOK 2

You Try It

Use the limit definition to find $f'(-1)$ for $f(x)=x^{2} + x - 2$.

Record the final result in a complete mathematical statement.

Answer: $-1$
Solution / scoring notes: Set up $\lim_{h\to0}\frac{f(-1+h)-f(-1)}{h}$. Expand, cancel the factor $h$, then let $h\qquad o0$; the value is $-1$.
U2-S2-NOTE-EX03
Section 2.2 | Notes Example | Current | DOK 2

Example

Use the table to estimate $f'(-1)$ with a symmetric difference.
$x$$f(x)$
$- \frac{11}{10}$$-2.88$
$-1$$-3.0$
$- \frac{9}{10}$$-3.08$

Organize the work so the rule, definition, or substitution can be followed line by line.

Answer: $-1.0$
Solution / scoring notes: Use $\frac{f(- \frac{9}{10})-f(- \frac{11}{10})}{\frac{1}{5}}$; the estimate is $-1.0$.
U2-S2-NOTE-YTI03

$x$$f(x)$
$\frac{9}{10}$$4.51$
$1$$5.0$
$\frac{11}{10}$$5.51$

Record the final result in a complete mathematical statement.

Answer: $5.0$
Solution / scoring notes: Use $\frac{f(\frac{11}{10})-f(\frac{9}{10})}{\frac{1}{5}}$; the estimate is $5.0$.
U2-S2-NOTE-EX04
Section 2.2 | Notes Example | Current | DOK 2

Example

For $f(x)=x \left(2 x + 3\right)$, write the tangent-line equation at $x=2$.

Organize the work so the rule, definition, or substitution can be followed line by line.

Answer: $y=11 x - 8$
Solution / scoring notes: Find $f(2)=14$ and $f'(2)=11$. Then use point-slope form to obtain $y=11 x - 8$.
U2-S2-NOTE-YTI04
Section 2.2 | Notes You Try It | Current | DOK 2

You Try It

For $f(x)=2 x^{2} + x + 2$, write the tangent-line equation at $x=1$.

Record the final result in a complete mathematical statement.

Answer: $y=5 x$
Solution / scoring notes: Find $f(1)=5$ and $f'(1)=5$. Then use point-slope form to obtain $y=5 x$.

Section 2.3 - Differentiability

U2-S3-NOTE-EX01
Section 2.3 | Notes Example | Current | DOK 2

Example

The graph of $f$ has a notable feature at $x=3$. Is $f$ differentiable at that input? Identify the feature that determines your answer.

Organize the work so the rule, definition, or substitution can be followed line by line.

Answer: No; the feature is a cusp.
Solution / scoring notes: A cusp prevents a finite two-sided derivative at $x=3$.
U2-S3-NOTE-YTI01
Section 2.3 | Notes You Try It | Current | DOK 2

You Try It

The graph of $f$ has a notable feature at $x=-2$. Is $f$ differentiable at that input? Identify the feature that determines your answer.

Record the final result in a complete mathematical statement.

Answer: No; the feature is a vertical tangent.
Solution / scoring notes: A vertical tangent prevents a finite two-sided derivative at $x=-2$.
U2-S3-NOTE-EX02
Section 2.3 | Notes Example | Current | DOK 2

Example

A function is continuous at $x=1$ and has a sharp corner there. Decide whether it is differentiable at $x=1$ and justify.

Organize the work so the rule, definition, or substitution can be followed line by line.

Answer: It is not differentiable there.
Solution / scoring notes: Continuity is necessary but not sufficient for differentiability. At a corner, the one-sided slopes do not agree.
U2-S3-NOTE-YTI02
Section 2.3 | Notes You Try It | Current | DOK 2

You Try It

A function is continuous at $x=-1$ and has a sharp corner there. Decide whether it is differentiable at $x=-1$ and justify.

Record the final result in a complete mathematical statement.

Answer: It is not differentiable there.
Solution / scoring notes: Continuity is necessary but not sufficient for differentiability. At a corner, the one-sided slopes do not agree.
U2-S3-NOTE-EX03
Section 2.3 | Notes Example | Current | DOK 2

Example

Consider $f(x)=\begin{cases}-3x+3,&x\le -2\\0x+9,&x\gt -2\end{cases}$. Is $f$ differentiable at $x=-2$?

Organize the work so the rule, definition, or substitution can be followed line by line.

Answer: No.
Solution / scoring notes: The pieces meet continuously, but the left slope is $-3$ and the right slope is $0$; unequal one-sided derivatives mean nondifferentiability.
U2-S3-NOTE-YTI03
Section 2.3 | Notes You Try It | Current | DOK 2

You Try It

Consider $f(x)=\begin{cases}3x+0,&x\le -1\\5x+2,&x\gt -1\end{cases}$. Is $f$ differentiable at $x=-1$?

Record the final result in a complete mathematical statement.

Answer: No.
Solution / scoring notes: The pieces meet continuously, but the left slope is $3$ and the right slope is $5$; unequal one-sided derivatives mean nondifferentiability.
U2-S3-NOTE-EX04
Section 2.3 | Notes Example | Current | DOK 1

Example

Which implication is always true for a real-valued function at an interior point of its domain?
  1. A vertical tangent implies a finite derivative.
  2. Differentiable implies continuous.
  3. A corner implies differentiability.
  4. Continuous implies differentiable.

Organize the work so the rule, definition, or substitution can be followed line by line.

Answer: B. Differentiable implies continuous.
Solution / scoring notes: Differentiability at a point guarantees continuity there. The converse is not always true.
U2-S3-NOTE-YTI04
Section 2.3 | Notes You Try It | Current | DOK 1

You Try It

Which implication is always true for a real-valued function at an interior point of its domain?
  1. A vertical tangent implies a finite derivative.
  2. Differentiable implies continuous.
  3. A corner implies differentiability.
  4. Continuous implies differentiable.

Record the final result in a complete mathematical statement.

Answer: B. Differentiable implies continuous.
Solution / scoring notes: Differentiability at a point guarantees continuity there. The converse is not always true.

Section 2.4 - Power and Basic Rules

U2-S4-NOTE-EX01
Section 2.4 | Notes Example | Current | DOK 1

Example

Differentiate $f(x)=- 5 x^{4} + 4 x^{2} - 7$.

Organize the work so the rule, definition, or substitution can be followed line by line.

Answer: $f'(x)=- 20 x^{3} + 8 x$
Solution / scoring notes: Apply the appropriate basic derivative rules term by term to get $f'(x)=- 20 x^{3} + 8 x$.
U2-S4-NOTE-YTI01
Section 2.4 | Notes You Try It | Current | DOK 1

You Try It

Differentiate $f(x)=\frac{3 \left(1 - x^{\frac{5}{2}}\right)}{x^{2}}$.

Record the final result in a complete mathematical statement.

Answer: $f'(x)=- \frac{6}{x^{3}} - \frac{3}{2 \sqrt{x}}$
Solution / scoring notes: Apply the appropriate basic derivative rules term by term to get $f'(x)=- \frac{6}{x^{3}} - \frac{3}{2 \sqrt{x}}$.
U2-S4-NOTE-EX02
Section 2.4 | Notes Example | Current | DOK 1

Example

Find the derivative of $y=- 2 x - 2 \cos{\left(x \right)}$.

Organize the work so the rule, definition, or substitution can be followed line by line.

Answer: $\frac{dy}{dx}=2 \sin{\left(x \right)} - 2$
Solution / scoring notes: Use the standard derivative for the core function and the sum/constant-multiple rules: $\frac{dy}{dx}=2 \sin{\left(x \right)} - 2$.
U2-S4-NOTE-YTI02
Section 2.4 | Notes You Try It | Current | DOK 1

You Try It

Find the derivative of $y=3 e^{x} + 2$.

Record the final result in a complete mathematical statement.

Answer: $\frac{dy}{dx}=3 e^{x}$
Solution / scoring notes: Use the standard derivative for the core function and the sum/constant-multiple rules: $\frac{dy}{dx}=3 e^{x}$.
U2-S4-NOTE-EX03
Section 2.4 | Notes Example | Current | DOK 2

Example

For $f(x)=- 2 x^{4} + 2 x + 4$, find the slope of the tangent line at $x=2$.

Organize the work so the rule, definition, or substitution can be followed line by line.

Answer: $-62$
Solution / scoring notes: Differentiate to get $f'(x)=2 - 8 x^{3}$ and evaluate at $x=2$: $-62$.
U2-S4-NOTE-YTI03
Section 2.4 | Notes You Try It | Current | DOK 2

You Try It

For $f(x)=2 x^{2} - 2 x + 1$, find the slope of the tangent line at $x=1$.

Record the final result in a complete mathematical statement.

Answer: $2$
Solution / scoring notes: Differentiate to get $f'(x)=4 x - 2$ and evaluate at $x=1$: $2$.
U2-S4-NOTE-EX04
Section 2.4 | Notes Example | Current | DOK 2

Example

A student differentiates $5x^5$ as $5x^4$. Identify the error and give the correct derivative.

Organize the work so the rule, definition, or substitution can be followed line by line.

Answer: $25 x^{4}$
Solution / scoring notes: The power rule multiplies by the exponent before reducing it by one, giving $25 x^{4}$.
U2-S4-NOTE-YTI04
Section 2.4 | Notes You Try It | Current | DOK 2

You Try It

A student differentiates $3x^3$ as $3x^2$. Identify the error and give the correct derivative.

Record the final result in a complete mathematical statement.

Answer: $9 x^{2}$
Solution / scoring notes: The power rule multiplies by the exponent before reducing it by one, giving $9 x^{2}$.

Section 2.5 - Product, Quotient, and Core Functions

U2-S5-NOTE-EX01
Section 2.5 | Notes Example | Current | DOK 2

Example

Differentiate $y=3 \left(x - 1\right) e^{x}$ using the product rule.

Organize the work so the rule, definition, or substitution can be followed line by line.

Answer: $\frac{dy}{dx}=3 x e^{x}$
Solution / scoring notes: Use $(uv)'=u'v+uv'$. Substitution simplifies to $3 x e^{x}$.
U2-S5-NOTE-YTI01
Section 2.5 | Notes You Try It | Current | DOK 2

You Try It

Differentiate $y=\left(x^{2} + 2 x + 4\right) \cos{\left(x \right)}$ using the product rule.

Record the final result in a complete mathematical statement.

Answer: $\frac{dy}{dx}=2 \left(x + 1\right) \cos{\left(x \right)} - \left(x^{2} + 2 x + 4\right) \sin{\left(x \right)}$
Solution / scoring notes: Use $(uv)'=u'v+uv'$. Substitution simplifies to $2 \left(x + 1\right) \cos{\left(x \right)} - \left(x^{2} + 2 x + 4\right) \sin{\left(x \right)}$.
U2-S5-NOTE-EX02
Section 2.5 | Notes Example | Current | DOK 2

Example

Find the derivative of $q(x)=\frac{x + e^{x}}{x^{2} + 4}$.

Organize the work so the rule, definition, or substitution can be followed line by line.

Answer: $q'(x)=\frac{- 2 x \left(x + e^{x}\right) + \left(x^{2} + 4\right) \left(e^{x} + 1\right)}{\left(x^{2} + 4\right)^{2}}$
Solution / scoring notes: Use $(u/v)'=(u'v-uv')/v^2$; simplifying gives $\frac{- 2 x \left(x + e^{x}\right) + \left(x^{2} + 4\right) \left(e^{x} + 1\right)}{\left(x^{2} + 4\right)^{2}}$.
U2-S5-NOTE-YTI02
Section 2.5 | Notes You Try It | Current | DOK 2

You Try It

Find the derivative of $q(x)=\frac{1 - 4 x}{x^{2} - 3}$.

Record the final result in a complete mathematical statement.

Answer: $q'(x)=\frac{2 \left(2 x^{2} - x + 6\right)}{x^{4} - 6 x^{2} + 9}$
Solution / scoring notes: Use $(u/v)'=(u'v-uv')/v^2$; simplifying gives $\frac{2 \left(2 x^{2} - x + 6\right)}{x^{4} - 6 x^{2} + 9}$.
U2-S5-NOTE-EX03
Section 2.5 | Notes Example | Current | DOK 2

Example

Use the table to find $\left(\frac{f}{g}\right)'(1)$.
$x=1$
$f(x)$$-1$
$f'(x)$$2$
$g(x)$$-4$
$g'(x)$$3$

Organize the work so the rule, definition, or substitution can be followed line by line.

Answer: $- \frac{5}{16}$
Solution / scoring notes: Quotient rule gives $- \frac{5}{16}$.
U2-S5-NOTE-YTI03
Section 2.5 | Notes You Try It | Current | DOK 2

You Try It

Use the table to find $(fg)'(-1)$.
$x=-1$
$f(x)$$-4$
$f'(x)$$2$
$g(x)$$-4$
$g'(x)$$4$

Record the final result in a complete mathematical statement.

Answer: $-24$
Solution / scoring notes: Product rule: $f'(-1)g(-1)+f(-1)g'(-1)=-24$.
U2-S5-NOTE-EX04
Section 2.5 | Notes Example | Current | DOK 2

Example

For $f(x)=\left(x - 4\right) \left(x^{2} + 4\right)$, write the tangent-line equation at $x=1$.

Organize the work so the rule, definition, or substitution can be followed line by line.

Answer: $y=- x - 14$
Solution / scoring notes: Use the product rule to find $f'(1)=-1$ and $f(1)=-15$; point-slope form gives $y=- x - 14$.
U2-S5-NOTE-YTI04
Section 2.5 | Notes You Try It | Current | DOK 2

You Try It

For $f(x)=\left(- 3 x - 4\right) e^{x}$, write the tangent-line equation at $x=0$.

Record the final result in a complete mathematical statement.

Answer: $y=- 7 x - 4$
Solution / scoring notes: Use the product rule to find $f'(0)=-7$ and $f(0)=-4$; point-slope form gives $y=- 7 x - 4$.

U2-S1-PS1-C05
Section 2.1 | Practice Set 1 | Current | DOK 1
Write, but do not simplify, the difference quotient for the average rate of change of $f(x)=- 4 x^{2} + 3 x + 2$ from $x=3$ to $x=3+h$.

Show the setup before you simplify or evaluate.

Answer: $\frac{f(3+h)-f(3)}{h}$
Solution / scoring notes: The interval width is $h$, so subtract the two function values and divide by $h$.
U2-S1-PS1-L01
Section 2.1 | Practice Set 1 | Last | DOK 2
Evaluate $\lim_{x\to -6}\frac{x^{2} - 36}{x + 6}$.

Show the setup before you simplify or evaluate.

Answer: $-12$
Solution / scoring notes: Factor the difference of powers, cancel $x + 6$, and substitute $x=-6$ to obtain $-12$.
U2-S1-PS1-L02
Section 2.1 | Practice Set 1 | Last | DOK 1
For $f(x)=\frac{2}{x + 1}$, identify the vertical asymptote.

Show the setup before you simplify or evaluate.

Answer: $x=-1$
Solution / scoring notes: The denominator is zero at $x=-1$, producing an infinite limit there.
U2-S1-PS1-L03
Section 2.1 | Practice Set 1 | Last | DOK 2
A function $f$ is continuous on $[-6,1]$, with $f(-6)=-3$ and $f(1)=15$. What does the Intermediate Value Theorem guarantee about the equation $f(x)=9$?

Show the setup before you simplify or evaluate.

Answer: There is at least one $c\in(-6,1)$ with $f(c)=9$.
Solution / scoring notes: Because $9$ lies between the endpoint values and $f$ is continuous, the IVT guarantees at least one such input.
U2-S1-PS1-L04
Section 2.1 | Practice Set 1 | Last | DOK 1
At $x=-7$, suppose $\lim_{x\to -7^-}f(x)=-10$ and $\lim_{x\to -7^+}f(x)=-7$. Does $\lim_{x\to -7}f(x)$ exist? Explain briefly.

Show the setup before you simplify or evaluate.

Answer: No.
Solution / scoring notes: A two-sided limit exists only when the left- and right-hand limits agree; here they are different.
U2-S1-PS1-L05
Section 2.1 | Practice Set 1 | Last | DOK 1
For $r(x)=\frac{4 x^{2} + 7}{x^{2} + 8}$, find $\lim_{x\to\infty}r(x)$.

Show the setup before you simplify or evaluate.

Answer: $4$
Solution / scoring notes: The numerator and denominator have the same degree, so the limit is the ratio of leading coefficients, $4$.
U2-S1-PS1-M01
Section 2.1 | Practice Set 1 | Mastery | DOK 2
Compute the secant slope for $g(x)=3 x^{2} - x - 1$ between $x=-1$ and $x=3$.

Show the setup before you simplify or evaluate.

Answer: $5$
Solution / scoring notes: Use $\frac{g(b)-g(a)}{b-a}$. Substitution gives $5$.
U2-S1-PS1-M02
Section 2.1 | Practice Set 1 | Mastery | DOK 1
What is the rate of change between the points $(-8,-4)$ and $(4,2)$?

Show the setup before you simplify or evaluate.

Answer: $\frac{1}{2}$
Solution / scoring notes: Compute rise over run: $\frac{2--4}{4--8}=\frac{1}{2}$.
U2-S1-PS1-M03
Section 2.1 | Practice Set 1 | Mastery | DOK 2
The function $h(x)=\frac{x^{2} - 1}{x + 1}$ is defined for $x\ne -1$. What value should be assigned to $h(-1)$ so the extended function is continuous?

Show the setup before you simplify or evaluate.

Answer: $-2$
Solution / scoring notes: Cancel the factor $x + 1$ for $x\ne -1$. The simplified expression has limit $-2$ at $x=-1$.

If $p(-4)=-12$ and $q(-4)=4$, evaluate $(p+q)(-4)$.

Show the setup before you simplify or evaluate.

Answer: $-8$
Solution / scoring notes: Add the function values: $-12+4=-8$.

U2-S1-PS1-M10
Section 2.1 | Practice Set 1 | Mastery | DOK 2
The function $h(x)=\frac{x^{3} - 343}{x - 7}$ is defined for $x\ne 7$. What value should be assigned to $h(7)$ so the extended function is continuous?

Show the setup before you simplify or evaluate.

Answer: $147$
Solution / scoring notes: Cancel the factor $x - 7$ for $x\ne 7$. The simplified expression has limit $147$ at $x=7$.

Section 2.2 - Derivative Definition and Notation

U2-S2-PS1-C01
Section 2.2 | Practice Set 1 | Current | DOK 1
In words, what does $f'(6)$ represent?

Show the setup before you simplify or evaluate.

Answer: The instantaneous rate of change of $f$ at $x=6$, equivalently the slope of the tangent line there.
Solution / scoring notes: Derivative-at-a-point notation names a single tangent slope/instantaneous rate, not a product.
U2-S2-PS1-C02
Section 2.2 | Practice Set 1 | Current | DOK 2
Use the limit definition to find $f'(2)$ for $f(x)=2 x^{2} + 2 x + 4$.

Show the setup before you simplify or evaluate.

Answer: $10$
Solution / scoring notes: Set up $\lim_{h\to0}\frac{f(2+h)-f(2)}{h}$. Expand, cancel the factor $h$, then let $h\qquad o0$; the value is $10$.
U2-S2-PS1-C03
Section 2.2 | Practice Set 1 | Current | DOK 2
Use the table to estimate $f'(0)$ with a symmetric difference.
$x$$f(x)$
$- \frac{1}{10}$$4.11$
$0$$4.0$
$\frac{1}{10}$$3.91$

Show the setup before you simplify or evaluate.

Answer: $-1.0$
Solution / scoring notes: Use $\frac{f(\frac{1}{10})-f(- \frac{1}{10})}{\frac{1}{5}}$; the estimate is $-1.0$.
U2-S2-PS1-C04
Section 2.2 | Practice Set 1 | Current | DOK 2
For $f(x)=2 x^{2} + 3 x + 2$, write the tangent-line equation at $x=-2$.

Show the setup before you simplify or evaluate.

Answer: $y=- 5 x - 6$
Solution / scoring notes: Find $f(-2)=4$ and $f'(-2)=-5$. Then use point-slope form to obtain $y=- 5 x - 6$.
U2-S2-PS1-C05
Section 2.2 | Practice Set 1 | Current | DOK 2
The graph shows $f$ and a line $L$. Explain what must be true at $x=-3$ for $L$ to be the tangent line there.

Show the setup before you simplify or evaluate.

Answer: $L$ must pass through $( -3, f(-3) )$ and have slope $f'(-3)$.
Solution / scoring notes: A tangent line at a point shares the point with the curve and has slope equal to the derivative at that input.
U2-S2-PS1-L01
Section 2.2 | Practice Set 1 | Last | DOK 2
Compute the difference quotient for $f(x)=- 4 x^{2} - 4 x - 2$ from $0$ to $2$.

Show the setup before you simplify or evaluate.

Answer: $-12$
Solution / scoring notes: Use $\frac{f(b)-f(a)}{b-a}$. Substitution and simplification give $-12$.
U2-S2-PS1-L02
Section 2.2 | Practice Set 1 | Last | DOK 2
The table gives values near $x=-1$. Use the outer two rows to estimate the tangent slope at $x=-1$.
$x$$f(x)$
$- \frac{3}{2}$$-0.5$
$-1$$-2.0$
$- \frac{1}{2}$$-2.5$

Show the setup before you simplify or evaluate.

Answer: $-2$
Solution / scoring notes: Use the symmetric secant slope $\frac{f(- \frac{1}{2})-f(- \frac{3}{2})}{1}$; it equals $-2$.
U2-S2-PS1-L03
Section 2.2 | Practice Set 1 | Last | DOK 2
For $f(x)=2 x^{2} - 2 x - 2$ at $a=-1$, the right-hand secant slopes are shown. Estimate the instantaneous rate as $h$ shrinks.
$h$Secant slope on $[a,a+h]$
$1$$-4.0$
$\frac{1}{2}$$-5.0$
$\frac{1}{4}$$-5.5$

Show the setup before you simplify or evaluate.

Answer: Approximately $-6$.
Solution / scoring notes: The secant slopes approach $-6$; this is the tangent-slope estimate.
U2-S2-PS1-L04
Section 2.2 | Practice Set 1 | Last | DOK 2
A storage tank contains $V(t)=2 t^{2} + 2 t + 30$ liters after $t$ minutes. Find the average rate of change from $t=1$ to $t=3$, and state its units.

Show the setup before you simplify or evaluate.

Answer: $10$ liters per minute.
Solution / scoring notes: Compute $\frac{V(b)-V(a)}{b-a}$; the result is $10$. The units are liters per minute.
U2-S2-PS1-L05
Section 2.2 | Practice Set 1 | Last | DOK 1
Write, but do not simplify, the difference quotient for the average rate of change of $f(x)=- x^{3} + 4 x^{2} + 4$ from $x=2$ to $x=2+h$.

Show the setup before you simplify or evaluate.

Answer: $\frac{f(2+h)-f(2)}{h}$
Solution / scoring notes: The interval width is $h$, so subtract the two function values and divide by $h$.
U2-S2-PS1-M01

Show the setup before you simplify or evaluate.

Answer: $10$
Solution / scoring notes: Use $\frac{g(b)-g(a)}{b-a}$. Substitution gives $10$.
U2-S2-PS1-M02
Section 2.2 | Practice Set 1 | Mastery | DOK 1
Find the slope of the line through $(-4,-7)$ and $(2,-11)$.

Show the setup before you simplify or evaluate.

Answer: $- \frac{2}{3}$
Solution / scoring notes: Compute rise over run: $\frac{-11--7}{2--4}=- \frac{2}{3}$.

Section 2.3 - Differentiability

U2-S3-PS1-C01
Section 2.3 | Practice Set 1 | Current | DOK 2
The graph of $f$ has a notable feature at $x=2$. Is $f$ differentiable at that input? Identify the feature that determines your answer.

Show the setup before you simplify or evaluate.

Answer: No; the feature is a vertical tangent.
Solution / scoring notes: A vertical tangent prevents a finite two-sided derivative at $x=2$.
U2-S3-PS1-C02
Section 2.3 | Practice Set 1 | Current | DOK 2
A function is continuous at $x=3$ and has a sharp corner there. Decide whether it is differentiable at $x=3$ and justify.

Show the setup before you simplify or evaluate.

Answer: It is not differentiable there.
Solution / scoring notes: Continuity is necessary but not sufficient for differentiability. At a corner, the one-sided slopes do not agree.
U2-S3-PS1-C03
Section 2.3 | Practice Set 1 | Current | DOK 2
Consider $f(x)=\begin{cases}3x+-2,&x\le 1\\6x+-5,&x\gt 1\end{cases}$. Is $f$ differentiable at $x=1$?

Show the setup before you simplify or evaluate.

Answer: No.
Solution / scoring notes: The pieces meet continuously, but the left slope is $3$ and the right slope is $6$; unequal one-sided derivatives mean nondifferentiability.
U2-S3-PS1-C04
Section 2.3 | Practice Set 1 | Current | DOK 1
Which implication is always true for a real-valued function at an interior point of its domain?
  1. Continuous implies differentiable.
  2. A corner implies differentiability.
  3. Differentiable implies continuous.
  4. A vertical tangent implies a finite derivative.

Show the setup before you simplify or evaluate.

Answer: C. Differentiable implies continuous.
Solution / scoring notes: Differentiability at a point guarantees continuity there. The converse is not always true.
U2-S3-PS1-C05
Section 2.3 | Practice Set 1 | Current | DOK 1
At $x=3$, a graph is continuous, but its tangent becomes vertical. What conclusion can you make about the ordinary derivative $f'(3)$?

Show the setup before you simplify or evaluate.

Answer: The ordinary derivative does not exist as a finite real number.
Solution / scoring notes: A vertical tangent corresponds to an unbounded slope rather than a finite derivative.

Section 2.4 - Power and Basic Rules

U2-S4-PS1-C01
Section 2.4 | Practice Set 1 | Current | DOK 1
Differentiate $f(x)=4 x^{2} + 4 e^{x}$.

Show the setup before you simplify or evaluate.

Answer: $f'(x)=8 x + 4 e^{x}$
Solution / scoring notes: Apply the appropriate basic derivative rules term by term to get $f'(x)=8 x + 4 e^{x}$.
U2-S4-PS1-C02
Section 2.4 | Practice Set 1 | Current | DOK 1
Find the derivative of $y=2 x - 3 \cos{\left(x \right)}$.

Show the setup before you simplify or evaluate.

Answer: $\frac{dy}{dx}=3 \sin{\left(x \right)} + 2$
Solution / scoring notes: Use the standard derivative for the core function and the sum/constant-multiple rules: $\frac{dy}{dx}=3 \sin{\left(x \right)} + 2$.
U2-S4-PS1-C03
Section 2.4 | Practice Set 1 | Current | DOK 2
For $f(x)=x \left(2 x^{2} - 4 x + 5\right)$, find the slope of the tangent line at $x=1$.

Show the setup before you simplify or evaluate.

Answer: $3$
Solution / scoring notes: Differentiate to get $f'(x)=6 x^{2} - 8 x + 5$ and evaluate at $x=1$: $3$.
U2-S4-PS1-C04
Section 2.4 | Practice Set 1 | Current | DOK 2
A student differentiates $3x^4$ as $3x^3$. Identify the error and give the correct derivative.

Show the setup before you simplify or evaluate.

Answer: $12 x^{3}$
Solution / scoring notes: The power rule multiplies by the exponent before reducing it by one, giving $12 x^{3}$.
U2-S4-PS1-C05
Section 2.4 | Practice Set 1 | Current | DOK 2
Rewrite with powers if helpful, then differentiate $g(x)=\frac{4 - 3 x^{\frac{5}{2}}}{x^{2}}$.

Show the setup before you simplify or evaluate.

Answer: $g'(x)=- \frac{8}{x^{3}} - \frac{3}{2 \sqrt{x}}$
Solution / scoring notes: Apply the power rule to negative and fractional exponents. The result is $g'(x)=- \frac{8}{x^{3}} - \frac{3}{2 \sqrt{x}}$.

Section 2.5 - Product, Quotient, and Core Functions

U2-S5-PS1-C01
Section 2.5 | Practice Set 1 | Current | DOK 2
Differentiate $y=\left(x^{2} - 3 x + 2\right) \cos{\left(x \right)}$ using the product rule.

Show the setup before you simplify or evaluate.

Answer: $\frac{dy}{dx}=\left(2 x - 3\right) \cos{\left(x \right)} + \left(- x^{2} + 3 x - 2\right) \sin{\left(x \right)}$
Solution / scoring notes: Use $(uv)'=u'v+uv'$. Substitution simplifies to $\left(2 x - 3\right) \cos{\left(x \right)} + \left(- x^{2} + 3 x - 2\right) \sin{\left(x \right)}$.
U2-S5-PS1-C02
Section 2.5 | Practice Set 1 | Current | DOK 2
Find the derivative of $q(x)=\frac{x^{2} + 3 x - 3}{x + 4}$.

Show the setup before you simplify or evaluate.

Answer: $q'(x)=\frac{x^{2} + 8 x + 15}{x^{2} + 8 x + 16}$
Solution / scoring notes: Use $(u/v)'=(u'v-uv')/v^2$; simplifying gives $\frac{x^{2} + 8 x + 15}{x^{2} + 8 x + 16}$.
U2-S5-PS1-C03
Section 2.5 | Practice Set 1 | Current | DOK 2
Use the table to find $\left(\frac{f}{g}\right)'(2)$.
$x=2$
$f(x)$$5$
$f'(x)$$-1$
$g(x)$$3$
$g'(x)$$-1$

Show the setup before you simplify or evaluate.

Answer: $\frac{2}{9}$
Solution / scoring notes: Quotient rule gives $\frac{2}{9}$.
U2-S5-PS1-C04
Section 2.5 | Practice Set 1 | Current | DOK 2
For $f(x)=- 8 \left(x + 1\right) \left(x^{2} + 1\right)$, write the tangent-line equation at $x=0$.

Show the setup before you simplify or evaluate.

Answer: $y=- 8 x - 8$
Solution / scoring notes: Use the product rule to find $f'(0)=-8$ and $f(0)=-8$; point-slope form gives $y=- 8 x - 8$.
U2-S5-PS1-C05
Section 2.5 | Practice Set 1 | Current | DOK 2
Before differentiating $r(x)=x^{2} + 4 x + 3$, choose an efficient strategy and find $r'(x)$.

Show the setup before you simplify or evaluate.

Answer: $r'(x)=2 x + 4$
Solution / scoring notes: Simplify first: $r(x)=x^{2} + 4 x + 3$. Then the basic rules give $r'(x)=2 x + 4$; the quotient rule is valid but unnecessarily long.
U2-S5-PS1-L01
Section 2.5 | Practice Set 1 | Last | DOK 1

Show the setup before you simplify or evaluate.

Answer: $f'(x)=8 x \left(- x^{2} - 1\right)$
Solution / scoring notes: Apply the appropriate basic derivative rules term by term to get $f'(x)=8 x \left(- x^{2} - 1\right)$.

Section 2.3 - Differentiability

U2-S3-PS2-C01
Section 2.3 | Practice Set 2 | Current | DOK 2
The graph of $f$ has a notable feature at $x=3$. Is $f$ differentiable at that input? Identify the feature that determines your answer.

Keep the key rule or definition visible in your work.

Answer: No; the feature is a cusp.
Solution / scoring notes: A cusp prevents a finite two-sided derivative at $x=3$.
U2-S3-PS2-C02
Section 2.3 | Practice Set 2 | Current | DOK 2
A function is continuous at $x=1$ and has a sharp corner there. Decide whether it is differentiable at $x=1$ and justify.

Keep the key rule or definition visible in your work.

Answer: It is not differentiable there.
Solution / scoring notes: Continuity is necessary but not sufficient for differentiability. At a corner, the one-sided slopes do not agree.
U2-S3-PS2-C03
Section 2.3 | Practice Set 2 | Current | DOK 2
Consider $f(x)=\begin{cases}3x+0,&x\le -2\\6x+6,&x\gt -2\end{cases}$. Is $f$ differentiable at $x=-2$?

Keep the key rule or definition visible in your work.

Answer: No.
Solution / scoring notes: The pieces meet continuously, but the left slope is $3$ and the right slope is $6$; unequal one-sided derivatives mean nondifferentiability.
U2-S3-PS2-C04
Section 2.3 | Practice Set 2 | Current | DOK 1
Which implication is always true for a real-valued function at an interior point of its domain?
  1. Continuous implies differentiable.
  2. Differentiable implies continuous.
  3. A vertical tangent implies a finite derivative.
  4. A corner implies differentiability.

Keep the key rule or definition visible in your work.

Answer: B. Differentiable implies continuous.
Solution / scoring notes: Differentiability at a point guarantees continuity there. The converse is not always true.
U2-S3-PS2-C05

Keep the key rule or definition visible in your work.

Answer: The ordinary derivative does not exist as a finite real number.
Solution / scoring notes: A vertical tangent corresponds to an unbounded slope rather than a finite derivative.

The function $h(x)=\frac{x^{4} - 1}{x + 1}$ is defined for $x\ne -1$. What value should be assigned to $h(-1)$ so the extended function is continuous?

Keep the key rule or definition visible in your work.

Answer: $-4$
Solution / scoring notes: Cancel the factor $x + 1$ for $x\ne -1$. The simplified expression has limit $-4$ at $x=-1$.
U2-S3-PS2-M05
Section 2.3 | Practice Set 2 | Mastery | DOK 1
Given $p(-6)=5$ and $q(-6)=6$, find $(p-q)(-6)$.

Keep the key rule or definition visible in your work.

Answer: $-1$
Solution / scoring notes: Subtract the function values: $5-(6)=-1$.

Section 2.4 - Power and Basic Rules

U2-S4-PS2-C01
Section 2.4 | Practice Set 2 | Current | DOK 1
Differentiate $f(x)=- 5 \sin{\left(x \right)} + 3 \cos{\left(x \right)}$.

Keep the key rule or definition visible in your work.

Answer: $f'(x)=- 3 \sin{\left(x \right)} - 5 \cos{\left(x \right)}$
Solution / scoring notes: Apply the appropriate basic derivative rules term by term to get $f'(x)=- 3 \sin{\left(x \right)} - 5 \cos{\left(x \right)}$.
U2-S4-PS2-C02
Section 2.4 | Practice Set 2 | Current | DOK 1
Find the derivative of $y=3 x - 3 \log{\left(x \right)}$.

Keep the key rule or definition visible in your work.

Answer: $\frac{dy}{dx}=3 - \frac{3}{x}$
Solution / scoring notes: Use the standard derivative for the core function and the sum/constant-multiple rules: $\frac{dy}{dx}=3 - \frac{3}{x}$.
U2-S4-PS2-C03
Section 2.4 | Practice Set 2 | Current | DOK 2
For $f(x)=- 3 x^{4} - 3 x - 2$, find the slope of the tangent line at $x=1$.

Keep the key rule or definition visible in your work.

Answer: $-15$
Solution / scoring notes: Differentiate to get $f'(x)=- 12 x^{3} - 3$ and evaluate at $x=1$: $-15$.
U2-S4-PS2-C04
Section 2.4 | Practice Set 2 | Current | DOK 2
A student differentiates $-4x^5$ as $-4x^4$. Identify the error and give the correct derivative.

Keep the key rule or definition visible in your work.

Answer: $- 20 x^{4}$
Solution / scoring notes: The power rule multiplies by the exponent before reducing it by one, giving $- 20 x^{4}$.

Rewrite with powers if helpful, then differentiate $g(x)=\frac{2 x^{\frac{9}{2}} + 3}{x^{4}}$.

Keep the key rule or definition visible in your work.

Answer: $g'(x)=- \frac{12}{x^{5}} + \frac{1}{\sqrt{x}}$
Solution / scoring notes: Apply the power rule to negative and fractional exponents. The result is $g'(x)=- \frac{12}{x^{5}} + \frac{1}{\sqrt{x}}$.
U2-S4-PS2-L01
Section 2.4 | Practice Set 2 | Last | DOK 2
The graph of $f$ has a notable feature at $x=-1$. Is $f$ differentiable at that input? Identify the feature that determines your answer.

Keep the key rule or definition visible in your work.

Answer: No; the feature is a cusp.
Solution / scoring notes: A cusp prevents a finite two-sided derivative at $x=-1$.
U2-S4-PS2-L02
Section 2.4 | Practice Set 2 | Last | DOK 2
A function is continuous at $x=1$ and has a sharp corner there. Decide whether it is differentiable at $x=1$ and justify.

Keep the key rule or definition visible in your work.

Use a complete sentence when the response is an interpretation. Focus the response on the corner reasoning requested here.

Answer: It is not differentiable there.
Solution / scoring notes: Continuity is necessary but not sufficient for differentiability. At a corner, the one-sided slopes do not agree.
U2-S4-PS2-L03
Section 2.4 | Practice Set 2 | Last | DOK 2
Consider $f(x)=\begin{cases}-3x+3,&x\le 0\\0x+3,&x\gt 0\end{cases}$. Is $f$ differentiable at $x=0$?

Keep the key rule or definition visible in your work.

Answer: No.
Solution / scoring notes: The pieces meet continuously, but the left slope is $-3$ and the right slope is $0$; unequal one-sided derivatives mean nondifferentiability.
U2-S4-PS2-L04
Section 2.4 | Practice Set 2 | Last | DOK 1
Which implication is always true for a real-valued function at an interior point of its domain?
  1. Continuous implies differentiable.
  2. Differentiable implies continuous.
  3. A corner implies differentiability.
  4. A vertical tangent implies a finite derivative.

Keep the key rule or definition visible in your work.

Answer: B. Differentiable implies continuous.
Solution / scoring notes: Differentiability at a point guarantees continuity there. The converse is not always true.
U2-S4-PS2-L05
Section 2.4 | Practice Set 2 | Last | DOK 1
At $x=4$, a graph is continuous, but its tangent becomes vertical. What conclusion can you make about the ordinary derivative $f'(4)$?

Keep the key rule or definition visible in your work.

Answer: The ordinary derivative does not exist as a finite real number.
Solution / scoring notes: A vertical tangent corresponds to an unbounded slope rather than a finite derivative.

Section 2.5 - Product, Quotient, and Core Functions

U2-S5-PS2-C01
Section 2.5 | Practice Set 2 | Current | DOK 2
Differentiate $y=\left(x^{2} - 4\right) \sin{\left(x \right)}$ using the product rule.

Keep the key rule or definition visible in your work.

Answer: $\frac{dy}{dx}=2 x \sin{\left(x \right)} + \left(x^{2} - 4\right) \cos{\left(x \right)}$
Solution / scoring notes: Use $(uv)'=u'v+uv'$. Substitution simplifies to $2 x \sin{\left(x \right)} + \left(x^{2} - 4\right) \cos{\left(x \right)}$.
U2-S5-PS2-C02
Section 2.5 | Practice Set 2 | Current | DOK 2
Find the derivative of $q(x)=\frac{- 3 x^{2} + 4 x + 3}{x - 4}$.

Keep the key rule or definition visible in your work.

Answer: $q'(x)=\frac{- 3 x^{2} + 24 x - 19}{x^{2} - 8 x + 16}$
Solution / scoring notes: Use $(u/v)'=(u'v-uv')/v^2$; simplifying gives $\frac{- 3 x^{2} + 24 x - 19}{x^{2} - 8 x + 16}$.

$x=-2$
$f(x)$$0$
$f'(x)$$2$
$g(x)$$-4$
$g'(x)$$2$

Keep the key rule or definition visible in your work.

Answer: $- \frac{1}{2}$
Solution / scoring notes: Quotient rule gives $- \frac{1}{2}$.

Keep the key rule or definition visible in your work.

Answer: $y=- x - 1$
Solution / scoring notes: Use the product rule to find $f'(-1)=-1$ and $f(-1)=0$; point-slope form gives $y=- x - 1$.

Section 2.5 | Practice Set 2 | Current | DOK 2
Before differentiating $r(x)=x^{4} + 3 x + 3$, choose an efficient strategy and find $r'(x)$.

Keep the key rule or definition visible in your work.

Answer: $r'(x)=4 x^{3} + 3$
Solution / scoring notes: Simplify first: $r(x)=x^{4} + 3 x + 3$. Then the basic rules give $r'(x)=4 x^{3} + 3$; the quotient rule is valid but unnecessarily long.

Section 2.5 - Product, Quotient, and Core Functions

U2-S5-PS3-C01
Section 2.5 | Practice Set 3 | Current | DOK 2
Differentiate $F(x)=\left(x^{2} - 3 x - 3\right) \cos{\left(x \right)}$.

Give the requested target in a concise match-ready form.

Answer: $F'(x)=\left(2 x - 3\right) \cos{\left(x \right)} + \left(- x^{2} + 3 x + 3\right) \sin{\left(x \right)}$
Solution / scoring notes: Product rule gives $F'(x)=\left(2 x - 3\right) \cos{\left(x \right)} + \left(- x^{2} + 3 x + 3\right) \sin{\left(x \right)}$.
U2-S5-PS3-C02
Section 2.5 | Practice Set 3 | Current | DOK 2
Differentiate $F(x)=\left(x + 2\right) e^{x}$.

Give the requested target in a concise match-ready form.

Answer: $F'(x)=\left(x + 3\right) e^{x}$
Solution / scoring notes: Product rule gives $F'(x)=\left(x + 3\right) e^{x}$.

Solution / scoring notes: Product rule gives $F'(x)=\left(2 x + 1\right) \cos{\left(x \right)} - \left(x^{2} + x + 4\right) \sin{\left(x \right)}$.

Extra Practice

Section 2.1 - Average to Instant Rate

U2-S1-XP-C01
Section 2.1 | Extra Practice | Current | DOK 2
Compute the difference quotient for $f(x)=4 x^{2} - x + 1$ from $1$ to $4$.

Write the final quantity with notation that matches the prompt.

Answer: $19$
Solution / scoring notes: Use $\frac{f(b)-f(a)}{b-a}$. Substitution and simplification give $19$.
U2-S1-XP-C02
Section 2.1 | Extra Practice | Current | DOK 2
The table gives values near $x=2$. Use the outer two rows to estimate the tangent slope at $x=2$.
$x$$f(x)$
$\frac{7}{4}$$7.875$
$2$$10.0$
$\frac{9}{4}$$12.375$

Write the final quantity with notation that matches the prompt.

Answer: $9$
Solution / scoring notes: Use the symmetric secant slope $\frac{f(\frac{9}{4})-f(\frac{7}{4})}{\frac{1}{2}}$; it equals $9$.
U2-S1-XP-C03
Section 2.1 | Extra Practice | Current | DOK 2
For $f(x)=x^{2} + 3 x - 2$ at $a=1$, the right-hand secant slopes are shown. Estimate the instantaneous rate as $h$ shrinks.
$h$Secant slope on $[a,a+h]$
$1$$6.0$
$\frac{1}{2}$$5.5$
$\frac{1}{4}$$5.25$

Write the final quantity with notation that matches the prompt.

Answer: Approximately $5$.
Solution / scoring notes: The secant slopes approach $5$; this is the tangent-slope estimate.
U2-S1-XP-C04
Section 2.1 | Extra Practice | Current | DOK 2
A storage tank contains $V(t)=2 t^{2} + 3 t + 30$ liters after $t$ minutes. Find the average rate of change from $t=1$ to $t=3$, and state its units.

Write the final quantity with notation that matches the prompt.

Answer: $11$ liters per minute.
Solution / scoring notes: Compute $\frac{V(b)-V(a)}{b-a}$; the result is $11$. The units are liters per minute.
U2-S1-XP-C05
Section 2.1 | Extra Practice | Current | DOK 1
Write, but do not simplify, the difference quotient for the average rate of change of $f(x)=- 3 x^{3} + 4 x^{2} + 5$ from $x=1$ to $x=1+h$.

Write the final quantity with notation that matches the prompt.

Answer: $\frac{f(1+h)-f(1)}{h}$
Solution / scoring notes: The interval width is $h$, so subtract the two function values and divide by $h$.
U2-S1-XP-L01
Section 2.1 | Extra Practice | Last | DOK 2
Evaluate $\lim_{x\to -2}\frac{x^{2} - 4}{x + 2}$.

Write the final quantity with notation that matches the prompt.

Answer: $-4$
Solution / scoring notes: Factor the difference of powers, cancel $x + 2$, and substitute $x=-2$ to obtain $-4$.
U2-S1-XP-L02
Section 2.1 | Extra Practice | Last | DOK 1
For $f(x)=\frac{1}{x - 3}$, identify the vertical asymptote.

Write the final quantity with notation that matches the prompt.

Answer: $x=3$
Solution / scoring notes: The denominator is zero at $x=3$, producing an infinite limit there.
U2-S1-XP-L03
Section 2.1 | Extra Practice | Last | DOK 2
A function $f$ is continuous on $[-8,3]$, with $f(-8)=-8$ and $f(3)=1$. What does the Intermediate Value Theorem guarantee about the equation $f(x)=-2$?

Write the final quantity with notation that matches the prompt.

Answer: There is at least one $c\in(-8,3)$ with $f(c)=-2$.
Solution / scoring notes: Because $-2$ lies between the endpoint values and $f$ is continuous, the IVT guarantees at least one such input.
U2-S1-XP-L04
Section 2.1 | Extra Practice | Last | DOK 1
At $x=-8$, suppose $\lim_{x\to -8^-}f(x)=8$ and $\lim_{x\to -8^+}f(x)=11$. Does $\lim_{x\to -8}f(x)$ exist? Explain briefly.

Write the final quantity with notation that matches the prompt.

Answer: No.
Solution / scoring notes: A two-sided limit exists only when the left- and right-hand limits agree; here they are different.
U2-S1-XP-L05
Section 2.1 | Extra Practice | Last | DOK 1
For $r(x)=\frac{5 x^{3} + 8}{2 x^{3} + 3}$, find $\lim_{x\to\infty}r(x)$.

Write the final quantity with notation that matches the prompt.

Answer: $\frac{5}{2}$
Solution / scoring notes: The numerator and denominator have the same degree, so the limit is the ratio of leading coefficients, $\frac{5}{2}$.

Section 2.2 - Derivative Definition and Notation

U2-S2-XP-C01
Section 2.2 | Extra Practice | Current | DOK 1
In words, what does $f'(2)$ represent?

Write the final quantity with notation that matches the prompt.

Answer: The instantaneous rate of change of $f$ at $x=2$, equivalently the slope of the tangent line there.
Solution / scoring notes: Derivative-at-a-point notation names a single tangent slope/instantaneous rate, not a product.
U2-S2-XP-C02
Section 2.2 | Extra Practice | Current | DOK 2
Use the limit definition to find $f'(1)$ for $f(x)=x^{2} - x - 2$.

Write the final quantity with notation that matches the prompt.

Answer: $1$
Solution / scoring notes: Set up $\lim_{h\to0}\frac{f(1+h)-f(1)}{h}$. Expand, cancel the factor $h$, then let $h\qquad o0$; the value is $1$.
U2-S2-XP-C03
Section 2.2 | Extra Practice | Current | DOK 2
Use the table to estimate $f'(2)$ with a symmetric difference.
$x$$f(x)$
$\frac{19}{10}$$3.32$
$2$$4.0$
$\frac{21}{10}$$4.72$

Write the final quantity with notation that matches the prompt.

Answer: $7.0$
Solution / scoring notes: Use $\frac{f(\frac{21}{10})-f(\frac{19}{10})}{\frac{1}{5}}$; the estimate is $7.0$.
U2-S2-XP-C04
Section 2.2 | Extra Practice | Current | DOK 2
For $f(x)=2 x \left(x - 1\right)$, write the tangent-line equation at $x=-1$.

Write the final quantity with notation that matches the prompt.

Answer: $y=- 6 x - 2$
Solution / scoring notes: Find $f(-1)=4$ and $f'(-1)=-6$. Then use point-slope form to obtain $y=- 6 x - 2$.
U2-S2-XP-C05
Section 2.2 | Extra Practice | Current | DOK 2
The graph shows $f$ and a line $L$. Explain what must be true at $x=-1$ for $L$ to be the tangent line there.

Write the final quantity with notation that matches the prompt.

Answer: $L$ must pass through $( -1, f(-1) )$ and have slope $f'(-1)$.
Solution / scoring notes: A tangent line at a point shares the point with the curve and has slope equal to the derivative at that input.

Write the final quantity with notation that matches the prompt.

Answer: $1$
Solution / scoring notes: Use $\frac{f(b)-f(a)}{b-a}$. Substitution and simplification give $1$.

Answer: $11$ liters per minute.

Answer: $\frac{f(-2+h)-f(-2)}{h}$
Solution / scoring notes: The interval width is $h$, so subtract the two function values and divide by $h$.

Section 2.3 - Differentiability

U2-S3-XP-C01
Section 2.3 | Extra Practice | Current | DOK 2
The graph of $f$ has a notable feature at $x=-2$. Is $f$ differentiable at that input? Identify the feature that determines your answer.

Write the final quantity with notation that matches the prompt.

Answer: No; the feature is a jump.
Solution / scoring notes: A jump prevents a finite two-sided derivative at $x=-2$.
U2-S3-XP-C02
Section 2.3 | Extra Practice | Current | DOK 2
A function is continuous at $x=-3$ and has a sharp corner there. Decide whether it is differentiable at $x=-3$ and justify.

Write the final quantity with notation that matches the prompt.

Answer: It is not differentiable there.
Solution / scoring notes: Continuity is necessary but not sufficient for differentiability. At a corner, the one-sided slopes do not agree.
U2-S3-XP-C03
Section 2.3 | Extra Practice | Current | DOK 2
Consider $f(x)=\begin{cases}-3x+-2,&x\le -2\\-2x+0,&x\gt -2\end{cases}$. Is $f$ differentiable at $x=-2$?

Write the final quantity with notation that matches the prompt.

Answer: No.
Solution / scoring notes: The pieces meet continuously, but the left slope is $-3$ and the right slope is $-2$; unequal one-sided derivatives mean nondifferentiability.
U2-S3-XP-C04
Section 2.3 | Extra Practice | Current | DOK 1
Which implication is always true for a real-valued function at an interior point of its domain?
  1. Continuous implies differentiable.
  2. Differentiable implies continuous.
  3. A vertical tangent implies a finite derivative.
  4. A corner implies differentiability.

Write the final quantity with notation that matches the prompt.

Answer: B. Differentiable implies continuous.
Solution / scoring notes: Differentiability at a point guarantees continuity there. The converse is not always true.
U2-S3-XP-C05
Section 2.3 | Extra Practice | Current | DOK 1
At $x=0$, a graph is continuous, but its tangent becomes vertical. What conclusion can you make about the ordinary derivative $f'(0)$?

Write the final quantity with notation that matches the prompt.

Answer: The ordinary derivative does not exist as a finite real number.
Solution / scoring notes: A vertical tangent corresponds to an unbounded slope rather than a finite derivative.

Section 2.4 - Power and Basic Rules

U2-S4-XP-C01
Section 2.4 | Extra Practice | Current | DOK 1
Differentiate $f(x)=\frac{4 x^{\frac{5}{2}} - 3}{x^{2}}$.

Write the final quantity with notation that matches the prompt.

Answer: $f'(x)=\frac{6}{x^{3}} + \frac{2}{\sqrt{x}}$
Solution / scoring notes: Apply the appropriate basic derivative rules term by term to get $f'(x)=\frac{6}{x^{3}} + \frac{2}{\sqrt{x}}$.
U2-S4-XP-C02
Section 2.4 | Extra Practice | Current | DOK 1
Find the derivative of $y=2 - 3 e^{x}$.

Write the final quantity with notation that matches the prompt.

Answer: $\frac{dy}{dx}=- 3 e^{x}$
Solution / scoring notes: Use the standard derivative for the core function and the sum/constant-multiple rules: $\frac{dy}{dx}=- 3 e^{x}$.
U2-S4-XP-C03
Section 2.4 | Extra Practice | Current | DOK 2

Write the final quantity with notation that matches the prompt.

Answer: $18$
Solution / scoring notes: Differentiate to get $f'(x)=4 x^{3} + 12 x^{2} + 2$ and evaluate at $x=1$: $18$.
U2-S4-XP-C04
Section 2.4 | Extra Practice | Current | DOK 2
A student differentiates $-3x^4$ as $-3x^3$. Identify the error and give the correct derivative.

Write the final quantity with notation that matches the prompt.

Answer: $- 12 x^{3}$
Solution / scoring notes: The power rule multiplies by the exponent before reducing it by one, giving $- 12 x^{3}$.
U2-S4-XP-C05
Section 2.4 | Extra Practice | Current | DOK 2
Rewrite with powers if helpful, then differentiate $g(x)=\frac{5 x^{\frac{7}{2}} + 4}{x^{3}}$.

Write the final quantity with notation that matches the prompt.

Answer: $g'(x)=- \frac{12}{x^{4}} + \frac{5}{2 \sqrt{x}}$
Solution / scoring notes: Apply the power rule to negative and fractional exponents. The result is $g'(x)=- \frac{12}{x^{4}} + \frac{5}{2 \sqrt{x}}$.

Write the final quantity with notation that matches the prompt.

Solution / scoring notes: Use $(u/v)'=(u'v-uv')/v^2$; simplifying gives $\frac{2 x \left(3 x - e^{x}\right) + \left(x^{2} + 3\right) \left(e^{x} - 3\right)}{\left(x^{2} + 3\right)^{2}}$.

Answer: No.
Solution / scoring notes: The pieces meet continuously, but the left slope is $-3$ and the right slope is $-2$; unequal one-sided derivatives mean nondifferentiability.

Section 2.3 - Differentiability

U2-S3-WU1-C01
Section 2.3 | Warm Up 1 | Current | DOK 2
A function is continuous at $x=1$ and has a sharp corner there. Decide whether it is differentiable at $x=1$ and justify.

Use a direct method and keep the response concise.

Answer: It is not differentiable there.
Solution / scoring notes: Continuity is necessary but not sufficient for differentiability. At a corner, the one-sided slopes do not agree.

Use a direct method and keep the response concise.

Answer: $5$
Solution / scoring notes: Set up $\lim_{h\to0}\frac{f(1+h)-f(1)}{h}$. Expand, cancel the factor $h$, then let $h\qquad o0$; the value is $5$.

Write the first mathematical step before carrying it out.

Answer: No.
Solution / scoring notes: The pieces meet continuously, but the left slope is $1$ and the right slope is $2$; unequal one-sided derivatives mean nondifferentiability.

Which implication is always true for a real-valued function at an interior point of its domain?

Solution / scoring notes: Differentiability at a point guarantees continuity there. The converse is not always true.

Section 2.5 - Product, Quotient, and Core Functions

U2-S5-WU1-C01
Section 2.5 | Warm Up 1 | Current | DOK 2
Find the derivative of $q(x)=\frac{x + 4}{x^{2} + 2}$.

Use a direct method and keep the response concise.

Answer: $q'(x)=\frac{x^{2} - 2 x \left(x + 4\right) + 2}{\left(x^{2} + 2\right)^{2}}$
Solution / scoring notes: Use $(u/v)'=(u'v-uv')/v^2$; simplifying gives $\frac{x^{2} - 2 x \left(x + 4\right) + 2}{\left(x^{2} + 2\right)^{2}}$.

Answer: No.

Which implication is always true for a real-valued function at an interior point of its domain?
  1. Differentiable implies continuous.
  2. A vertical tangent implies a finite derivative.
  3. Continuous implies differentiable.
  4. A corner implies differentiability.

Use precise notation in the final response.

Answer: A. Differentiable implies continuous.
Solution / scoring notes: Differentiability at a point guarantees continuity there. The converse is not always true.

Give the answer and one supporting mathematical step.

Answer: $\frac{dy}{dx}=- 48 x^{2} - 16 x + 12$
Solution / scoring notes: Use $(uv)'=u'v+uv'$. Substitution simplifies to $- 48 x^{2} - 16 x + 12$.

Answer: $q'(x)=\frac{3 x^{2} + 24 x + 7}{x^{2} + 8 x + 16}$
Solution / scoring notes: Use $(u/v)'=(u'v-uv')/v^2$; simplifying gives $\frac{3 x^{2} + 24 x + 7}{x^{2} + 8 x + 16}$.

Investigate the instantaneous rate of $f(x)=x^{3} - 2 x + 4$ at $x=1$. Use the secant-slope data, describe the trend, propose a tangent-slope estimate, and explain why the smallest interval alone should not be treated as proof.

$2$$11.0$
$1$$5.0$
$\frac{1}{2}$$2.75$
$\frac{1}{4}$$1.8125$

Scoring criteria: Uses at least three secant values; gives a justified estimate; connects limiting secant slope to instantaneous rate; discusses evidence quality.
Estimated student time: 12-15 minutes
Answer: $1$

$2$$0$

Section 2.3 - Differentiability

U2-S3-PERF01
Section 2.3 | Performance Future Task | Performance | DOK 4

Performance / Future Task

Stress-test the claim "every continuous function is differentiable." For each function below, classify continuity and differentiability at $x=0$, then revise the claim into a statement that is always true: $f(x)=|x|$, $g(x)=x^2$, $h(x)=\sqrt[3]{x}$, and $p(x)=\begin{cases}x+1,&x\lt 0\x-1,&x\ge0\end{cases}$.
Expected evidence: Classifies all four; cites local feature/condition; revises the claim precisely.
Scoring criteria: Classifies all four; cites local feature/condition; revises the claim precisely.
Estimated student time: 12-15 minutes
Answer: Revised claim: differentiability implies continuity, but continuity does not imply differentiability.
Solution / scoring notes: At 0: $f$ is continuous but not differentiable (corner); $g$ is continuous and differentiable; $h$ is continuous with a vertical tangent so no finite derivative; $p$ is discontinuous and not differentiable.

$\frac{1}{100}$$13.1612$

Build and evaluate a tangent-line model for $f(x)=\left(x - 2\right) \left(x^{2} + 1\right)$ at $x=1$. Use the product rule to derive the tangent line, then compare model predictions with actual values and describe how accuracy changes with distance from the tangency point.
$x$Actual $f(x)$Tangent model
$0.8$$- \frac{246}{125}$$-2$
$0.9$$- \frac{1991}{1000}$$-2$
$1.1$$- \frac{1989}{1000}$$-2$
$1.2$$- \frac{244}{125}$$-2$

Answer: $y=-2$
Solution / scoring notes: At $x=1$, $f(1)=-2$ and $f'(1)=0$, so the tangent line is $y=-2$. The model is closest near 1 and generally loses accuracy farther away.

Mixed Rule Selection

For each function, name the most direct rule and compute the derivative: $A(x)=- 4 \left(3 x + 4\right) \left(x^{2} - 1\right)$, $B(x)=\frac{\sin{\left(x \right)} + 1}{x + 3}$, $C(x)=\frac{3 - 4 x^{\frac{5}{2}}}{x^{2}}$.
Answer: A: product rule, $A'(x)=- 36 x^{2} - 32 x + 12$; B: quotient rule, $B'(x)=\frac{\left(x + 3\right) \cos{\left(x \right)} - \sin{\left(x \right)} - 1}{\left(x + 3\right)^{2}}$; C: basic rules, $C'(x)=- \frac{6}{x^{3}} - \frac{2}{\sqrt{x}}$.
Solution / scoring notes: Choose the rule from the outer structure, then differentiate accurately.

DOK 3 - Definition vs Rule Strategy

U2-FA-D3-01-V1
Section 2.5 | Formative Assessment | N-A | DOK 3

Definition vs Rule Strategy

Two valid approaches are available for finding $f'(1)$ when $f(x)=2 x^{3} + 2 x^{2} - 2 x + 2$: the limit definition and derivative rules. Compare the approaches. Which is more efficient here, and what conceptual information does the less efficient approach make visible? Support your choice by obtaining the derivative value.
Answer: $f'(1)=8$; rules are more efficient, while the limit definition displays the derivative as the limit of secant slopes.
Solution / scoring notes: A complete response computes the same value both conceptually and procedurally, then distinguishes efficiency from meaning.

DOK 3 - Differentiability Argument Critique

U2-FA-D3-02-V1
Section 2.3 | Formative Assessment | N-A | DOK 3

Differentiability Argument Critique

A student argues: "The graph is continuous with a corner at $x=2$, so continuity alone tells me the derivative exists." Critique the argument and replace it with a correct condition-based explanation.
Answer: The claim is false. Differentiability implies continuity, but continuity alone does not guarantee a finite derivative; local slope behavior must also agree and be finite.
Solution / scoring notes: A corner blocks differentiability. The repaired argument must distinguish the one-way implication.

U2-FA-D3-04-V1
Section 2.5 | Formative Assessment | N-A | DOK 3

Rule Selection and Structure Defense

For $r(x)=x^{4} + 2 x + 3$, one student immediately uses the quotient rule while another simplifies first. Defend the more efficient strategy, compute the derivative, and explain why the other strategy is valid but structurally inefficient.
Answer: $r'(x)=4 x^{3} + 2$; simplify first.
Solution / scoring notes: Since every numerator term contains $x$, simplify to $r(x)=x^{4} + 2 x + 3$. Basic rules then give $r'(x)=4 x^{3} + 2$. Quotient rule creates avoidable algebra but can still work.

DOK 4 - Differentiability Case Study

U2-FA-D4-02-V1
Section 2.3 | Formative Assessment | Performance | DOK 4

Differentiability Case Study

Case study: classify continuity and differentiability at $x=2$ for each function, test the claim "continuous means differentiable," and write a refined decision rule that handles all cases: $f(x)=|x - 2|$, $g(x)=\sqrt[3]{x - 2}$, $h(x)=(x - 2)^2$, and a function with a jump at $x=2$. Explain what evidence distinguishes a corner, vertical tangent, smooth point, and discontinuity.
Expected evidence: Performance evidence aligned to assessment-plan DOK 4 family.
Scoring criteria: Score for completion of all requested evidence, mathematical accuracy, and justified conclusions.
Estimated student time: 12-18 minutes
Answer: Only the smooth quadratic is differentiable at the focus point; the absolute-value corner and cube-root vertical tangent are continuous but not differentiable with a finite derivative; the jump is neither continuous nor differentiable.
Solution / scoring notes: A refined rule checks continuity first, then whether left/right local slopes agree and are finite. The task requires applying that rule across all four cases.
U2-FA-D4-02-V2
Section 2.3 | Formative Assessment | Performance | DOK 4

Differentiability Case Study

Case study: classify continuity and differentiability at $x=5$ for each function, test the claim "continuous means differentiable," and write a refined decision rule that handles all cases: $f(x)=|x - 5|$, $g(x)=\sqrt[3]{x - 5}$, $h(x)=(x - 5)^2$, and a function with a jump at $x=5$. Explain what evidence distinguishes a corner, vertical tangent, smooth point, and discontinuity.
Expected evidence: Performance evidence aligned to assessment-plan DOK 4 family.
Scoring criteria: Score for completion of all requested evidence, mathematical accuracy, and justified conclusions.
Estimated student time: 12-18 minutes
Answer: Only the smooth quadratic is differentiable at the focus point; the absolute-value corner and cube-root vertical tangent are continuous but not differentiable with a finite derivative; the jump is neither continuous nor differentiable.
Solution / scoring notes: A refined rule checks continuity first, then whether left/right local slopes agree and are finite. The task requires applying that rule across all four cases.

Evidence sourceValue
Symmetric numerical estimate$-5.00018$
Claimed rule result$-3$
Expected evidence: Performance evidence aligned to assessment-plan DOK 4 family.

$x$Actual $F(x)$Model value
$- \frac{6}{5}$$- \frac{184 \cos{\left(\frac{6}{5} \right)}}{25}$$- \frac{37 \cos{\left(1 \right)}}{5} + \frac{7 \sin{\left(1 \right)}}{5}$
$- \frac{11}{10}$$- \frac{719 \cos{\left(\frac{11}{10} \right)}}{100}$$- \frac{36 \cos{\left(1 \right)}}{5} + \frac{7 \sin{\left(1 \right)}}{10}$

The comparison should show best local agreement nearest the tangency point.

11. Summative Assessment Source Content

Summative Version 1

U2-SUM-V1-Q01
Section 2.1 | Summative Assessment | Unit-Synthesis | DOK 2

Unit 2 Summative - Version 1

What is the average rate of change of $f(x)=3 x^{2} - x - 2$ on $[-1,1]$?
  1. $-7$
  2. $-1$
  3. $5$
  4. $-2$
Answer: B. $-1$
Solution / scoring notes: Average rate is change in output divided by change in input.
U2-SUM-V1-Q02
Section 2.1 | Summative Assessment | Unit-Synthesis | DOK 2

Unit 2 Summative - Version 1

Use the table to estimate the instantaneous rate at $x=-1$.
$x$$f(x)$
$- \frac{11}{10}$$\frac{441}{100}$
$-1$$4$
$- \frac{9}{10}$$\frac{361}{100}$
  1. $-4$
  2. $-3$
  3. $-8$
  4. $-5$
Answer: A. $-4$
Solution / scoring notes: A symmetric secant through the closest values gives the best listed estimate.
U2-SUM-V1-Q03
Section 2.1 | Summative Assessment | Unit-Synthesis | DOK 1

Unit 2 Summative - Version 1

Which expression represents the instantaneous rate of change of $f$ at $x=1$?
  1. $\frac{f(1+h)-f(1)}{1+h}$
  2. $\lim_{h\to0}\frac{f(1+h)-f(1)}{h}$
  3. $\lim_{x\to 1}\frac{f(x)}{x - 1}$
  4. $\lim_{h\to0}\frac{f(1)-f(h)}{1-h}$
Answer: B. $\lim_{h\to0}\frac{f(1+h)-f(1)}{h}$
Solution / scoring notes: The derivative definition uses a changing increment h in both the input and denominator.
U2-SUM-V1-Q04
Section 2.1 | Summative Assessment | Unit-Synthesis | DOK 2

Unit 2 Summative - Version 1

A temperature function $T(t)$ is measured in degrees Celsius with $t$ in minutes. Explain the meaning of $T'(4)=7$ in context, including units.
Answer: At $t=4$ minutes, temperature is increasing at $7$ degrees Celsius per minute.
Solution / scoring notes: A derivative value is an instantaneous rate, so its units are output units per input unit.
U2-SUM-V1-Q05
Section 2.2 | Summative Assessment | Unit-Synthesis | DOK 1

Unit 2 Summative - Version 1

What does $f'(2)$ represent?
  1. the product $f\cdot 2$
  2. the slope of the tangent line to $f$ at $x=2$
  3. the average rate on every interval containing $2$
  4. the value $f(2)$
Answer: B. the slope of the tangent line to $f$ at $x=2$
Solution / scoring notes: Derivative-at-a-point notation represents the tangent slope/instantaneous rate at that input.
U2-SUM-V1-Q06
Section 2.2 | Summative Assessment | Unit-Synthesis | DOK 2

Unit 2 Summative - Version 1

Using the limit definition, compute $f'(1)$ for $f(x)=2 x^{2} - 2 x + 2$. Show the cancellation that makes the limit evaluable.
Answer: $2$
Solution / scoring notes: Set up $\lim_{h\to0}\frac{f(1+h)-f(1)}{h}$; expansion leaves a factor of $h$, which cancels, and the limit is $2$.

U2-SUM-V1-Q10
Section 2.3 | Summative Assessment | Unit-Synthesis | DOK 1

Unit 2 Summative - Version 1

Which statement is always true?
  1. A corner is compatible with differentiability.
  2. If a function is continuous at a point, it must be differentiable there.
  3. If a function is differentiable at a point, it must be continuous there.
  4. A vertical tangent guarantees a finite derivative.
Answer: C. If a function is differentiable at a point, it must be continuous there.
Solution / scoring notes: Differentiability implies continuity; the converse can fail.
U2-SUM-V1-Q11
Section 2.3 | Summative Assessment | Unit-Synthesis | DOK 2

Unit 2 Summative - Version 1

Determine whether $f(x)=\begin{cases}-1x+-2,&x\le 1\\0x+-3,&x\gt 1\end{cases}$ is differentiable at $x=1$. Justify using continuity and one-sided slopes.
Answer: No; it is continuous there but the one-sided slopes differ.
Solution / scoring notes: The pieces meet at the same value, but the left slope is $-1$ and the right slope is $0$.

Solution / scoring notes: A DOK 3 response must challenge the converse and state the valid one-way implication with a relevant counterexample.

Solution / scoring notes: The product rule requires both cross terms.

Which statement is always true?
  1. A corner is compatible with differentiability.
  2. If a function is continuous at a point, it must be differentiable there.
  3. If a function is differentiable at a point, it must be continuous there.
  4. A vertical tangent guarantees a finite derivative.

Place the requested quantity first in the final response. Focus the response on the continuity implication requested here.

Use the most specific vocabulary named by the representation. Focus the response on the continuity implication requested here.

Answer: C. If a function is differentiable at a point, it must be continuous there.
Solution / scoring notes: Differentiability implies continuity; the converse can fail.

Unit 2 Summative - Version 6

Determine whether $f(x)=\begin{cases}1x+2,&x\le 2\\3x+-2,&x\gt 2\end{cases}$ is differentiable at $x=2$. Justify using continuity and one-sided slopes.
Answer: No; it is continuous there but the one-sided slopes differ.
Solution / scoring notes: The pieces meet at the same value, but the left slope is $1$ and the right slope is $3$.

  1. $5 \cos{\left(x \right)} - 3$
  2. $x + 5 \cos{\left(x \right)} - 3$
  3. $3 - 5 \cos{\left(x \right)}$
  4. $5 \sin{\left(x \right)} - 3$
Answer: A. $5 \cos{\left(x \right)} - 3$
Solution / scoring notes: Use the core-function derivative and constant-multiple/sum rules.
U2-SUM-V8-Q15

Unit 2 Summative - Version 8

Find the tangent-line equation to $f(x)=x^{3} - 5 x + 5$ at $x=2$ using basic derivative rules.
Answer: $y=7 x - 11$
Solution / scoring notes: Compute $f(2)=3$ and $f'(2)=7$; point-slope form yields $y=7 x - 11$.

Section 2.4 | Summative Assessment | Unit-Synthesis | DOK 2

Unit 2 Summative - Version 8

A student claims $\frac{d}{dx}(-5x^3)=- 5 x^{2}$. Diagnose the error, correct the derivative, and state the rule that was misused.

Make the concluding notation unambiguous about the point being discussed. Focus the response on the power rule error requested here.

Answer: $- 15 x^{2}$
Solution / scoring notes: The exponent must multiply the coefficient before decreasing by 1. The correct power-rule result is $- 15 x^{2}$.

Unit 2 Summative - Version 8

What is the derivative of $F(x)=- 2 \left(2 x - 1\right) \left(2 x^{2} - 1\right)$?
  1. $- 24 x^{2} + 8 x + 4$
  2. $- 16 x$
  3. $8 x \left(1 - 2 x\right)$
  4. $4 - 8 x^{2}$
Answer: A. $- 24 x^{2} + 8 x + 4$
Solution / scoring notes: The product rule requires both cross terms.

Unit 2 Summative - Version 8

Which derivative is correct for $Q(x)=\frac{2 \left(2 x^{2} - x - 2\right)}{x + 1}$?
  1. $\frac{2 \left(2 x^{2} + 4 x + 1\right)}{x + 1}$
  2. $\frac{2 \left(2 x^{2} + 4 x + 1\right)}{x^{2} + 2 x + 1}$
  3. $8 x - 2$
  4. $\frac{2 \left(6 x^{2} + 2 x - 3\right)}{x^{2} + 2 x + 1}$
Answer: B. $\frac{2 \left(2 x^{2} + 4 x + 1\right)}{x^{2} + 2 x + 1}$
Solution / scoring notes: The quotient rule uses denominator squared and subtraction in the numerator.

Unit 2 Summative - Version 8

Using the table, compute both $(fg)'(-2)$ and $\left(\frac{f}{g}\right)'(-2)$.
$x=-2$
$f(x)$$0$
$f'(x)$$-3$
$g(x)$$-3$
$g'(x)$$-1$
Answer: $9$ and $1$
Solution / scoring notes: Product rule gives $9$; quotient rule gives $1$.
U2-SUM-V8-Q20
Section 2.5 | Summative Assessment | Unit-Synthesis | DOK 3

Unit 2 Summative - Version 8

Choose and defend an efficient derivative strategy for $R(x)=x^{2} + 3 x + 1$. Compute $R'(x)$ and explain why a different valid strategy would require more work.
Answer: $R'(x)=2 x + 3$
Solution / scoring notes: Simplify to $R(x)=x^{2} + 3 x + 1$, then use basic rules. This gives $R'(x)=2 x + 3$. Quotient rule is valid but structurally inefficient.
